The underlying graph of
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
, where most of the embedded Pauli basis game is hidden.

Question distribution of the baby question reduced game
:
With probability
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
do one of the following:

•

Sample an edge from
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
according to the appropriate distribution therein.

•

Sample the single edge
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}
from
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
.

•

Sample a uniformly random edge from the Sampling apparatus.

•

Sample a uniformly random edge from the Hiding apparatus.

Lengths and formal generating sets for the augmented vertices of baby question reduction
:

Sampling apparatus
— The readable length of
\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
(and
\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
) is
k+\Lambda
, and its unreadable length is
\Lambda
. We associate with it the formal generators

\begin{split}\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}=\{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,i}&\mid 1\leq i\leq k\}\;,\\
\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R}}=\{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R},j}\mid,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\}\;,&\quad\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}=\{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L},j}\mid,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\}\;,\\
S^{\mathfrak{R}}_{\mathtt{Sample}_{A}}=\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}\sqcup\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}\;,&\quad S^{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathtt{Sample}_{A}}=\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}\;.\end{split}

and similarly for
\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
. Namely, answers are formatted as
(z_{sam},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}})
, where
z_{sam}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, and
a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}
.

Hiding apparatus
—

•

The readable length of
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Read}_{B}
) is
r+\Lambda
, and its unreadable length is
k+\Lambda
. We associate with it the formal generators

\begin{split}\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A}=\{\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A,i}\mid 1\leq i\leq r\}\;,&\quad\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}=\{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A,i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k\}\;,\\
\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R}}=\{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R},j}\mid 1\leq j\leq\Lambda\}\;,&\quad\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}=\{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L},j}\mid 1\leq j\leq\Lambda\}\;,\\
S^{\mathfrak{R}}_{\mathtt{Read}_{A}}=\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A}\sqcup\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R}}\;,&\quad S^{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathtt{Read}_{A}}=\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}\sqcup\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}\;,\end{split}

and similarly for
\mathtt{Read}_{B}
. Namely, answers are formatted as
(\mathtt{x}_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\nu_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
, where
\mathtt{x}_{read}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}
and
\nu_{read}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
(and for
B
,
(\mathtt{y}_{read},b_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\mu_{read},b_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
in the appropriate spaces).

•

The readable length of
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}
) is
0
, and its unreadable length is
k
. We associate with it the formal generators

\begin{split}S^{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}&=\mathsf{Perp}^{A}=\{\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k\}\;,\end{split}

and similarly for
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}
. Namely, the answer is formatted as
\nu\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
(respectively
\mu\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
).

The decision process for the augmented edges of the baby question reduced game
:
53
53

53

This is essentially the description of the controlled linear constraints function.

•

Sampling apparatus
—

(1)

In case
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
) is sampled, then check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq k\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{P}\mathsf{Z}^{i})=\gamma(\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,i})\;.

In other words, if
z
is the answer to
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}
, then it checks that
z=z_{sam}
.

(2)

In case
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Intro}_{B}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
) is sampled, first check that

\forall 1\leq j\leq\Lambda\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\cdot,j})=\gamma(\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\cdot,j})\;,

and then check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq r\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathfrak{s}^{A}_{ij}\gamma(\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,j})\;.

In other words, if
(z_{sam},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
, and
(\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}
, then it checks that
\mathtt{x}=\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z_{sam}),a^{\mathfrak{R}}=a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}}
and
a^{\mathfrak{L}}=a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}}
.

•

Hiding apparatus
—

(1)

In case
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Intro}_{B}-\mathtt{Read}_{B}
) is sampled, check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq r,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\cdot,j})=\gamma(\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\cdot,j})\;,\quad\gamma(\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})=\gamma(\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A,i})\;.

In other words, if
(\mathtt{x}_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\nu_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, and
(\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}
, check that
\mathtt{x}_{read}=\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}}_{read}=a^{\mathfrak{R}}
and
a^{\mathfrak{L}}_{read}=a^{\mathfrak{L}}
.

(2)

In case
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}-\mathtt{Read}_{B}
) is sampled, check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq k\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i})=\gamma(\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A,i})\;.

In other words, if
(\mathtt{x}_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\nu_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, and
\nu
is the answer to
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}
, check that
\nu=\nu_{read}
.

(3)

In case
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
) is sampled, check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq k\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i})=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}_{ij}\gamma(\mathsf{PX}^{j})\;,

where
(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
was the matrix whose rows span
\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
.
In other words, if
\nu
is the answer to
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}
and
\chi
is the answer to
\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
, check that
\nu=(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}(\chi)
.

Remark 4.7

(Informal analysis of
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
)
.

Before proving rigorously that this game is complete and sound, let us discuss the role of the various checks described above in forcing strategies to behave appropriately in this augmented version of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}\oplus\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
. Similarly to the way the ultimate goal of all the checks in
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
was for the observables at
\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{Z}}}
and
\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
to induce a (specific) representation of the Pauli group, the ultimate goal of all the above checks is to force the strategy to play
honestly
(Definition
4.4
) when the copy of
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
is played — namely, it samples a seed
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
uniformly at random, calculates
\mathfrak{s}(z)=\mathtt{x}\mathtt{y}
, calculates
(a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})
depending only on
\mathtt{x}
and
(b^{\mathfrak{R}},b^{\mathfrak{L}})
depending only on
\mathtt{y}
, and finally replies with
(\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}},\mathtt{y},b^{\mathfrak{R}},b^{\mathfrak{L}})
.

This is achieved as follows: First, the copy of the Pauli basis game forces the answer
z
in
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}
to be uniformly distributed over
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, using the PVM (in representation form)
\rho^{\mathds{Z}}\otimes{\rm Id}
(and
\rho^{\mathds{X}}\otimes{\rm Id}
for
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}
), where
\rho
is the representation from Definition
3.68
. The checks
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
and
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
force
\mathtt{x}
to be
\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)
and similarly
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
and
\mathtt{Intro}_{B}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
force
\mathtt{y}
to be
\mathfrak{s}^{B}(z)
. Now,
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
and
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
force
a^{\cdot}=a_{sam}^{\cdot}=a_{read}^{\cdot}
, and similarly
\mathtt{Sample}_{B}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}-\mathtt{Read}_{B}
force
b^{\cdot}=b_{sam}^{\cdot}=b_{read}^{\cdot}
. Furthermore, since they are mutually measured with the seed
z
(in
\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
) and with
\nu_{read}
(in
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
), they are forced to commute with the observables associated with them.
The checks
\mathtt{Read}_{A}-\mathtt{Hide}_{A}
and
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}
, force
\nu_{read}=\nu=(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}(\chi)
. Hence, the observables of
\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\cdot,\cdot}
are forced to commute with
{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}
for every
\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
, and with
{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes v}\otimes{\rm Id}
for every
v\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.
The second commutation means that the mutual orthonormal eigenbasis for the observables of
\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\cdot,\cdot}
is of the form
\{{\bf 1}_{z}\otimes u_{z,t}\mid z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k},t\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2\Lambda}\}
, and the first commutation means that they act the same on
{\bf 1}_{z}\otimes u
and
{\mathds{X}}^{\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}\cdot{\bf 1}_{z}\otimes u={\bf 1}_{z+\alpha}\otimes u
. This means that the response
a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}}
is the same for every two
z
’s that differ by an element of
\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
, which implies that they depend only on their
\mathfrak{s}^{A}
-image, and this is what we wanted!

A reader may notice that to achieve the above goal, we could have dropped the
\mathtt{Sample}_{\cdot}
and
\mathtt{Hide}_{\cdot}
vertices and applied a more direct check (simplifying the augmentation). Though this is true, it will hinder the perfect completeness case, as we seek perfect
\mathsf{ZPC}
strategies, in particular strategies that commute along edges, which is problematic without these “buffer” questions.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\mathscr{B}
a tuple of
n
vectors in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
that induce an
[n,k,d]
-code, and
\mathfrak{G}
a game with the properties fixed in the beginning of this subsection. Then, the baby question reduction game
\mathfrak{Baby}(k,\mathscr{B},\mathfrak{G})=\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
has the following properties:

(1)

Perfect
\mathsf{ZPC}
Completeness
: If
\mathfrak{G}
has a perfect
\mathsf{ZPC}
strategy, then so does
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
.

(2)

Soundness
: If
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
has a strategy with value
1-\varepsilon
, then
\mathfrak{G}
has a strategy with value of at least
1-O(\sqrt{(1+\nicefrac{{k^{2}}}{{d^{2}}})\varepsilon})
.
54
54

54

The
O
-notation is genuinely some universal constant that can be extracted from all the approximations we are doing.

(3)

Entanglement
: For every
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathscr{E}(\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G}),1-\varepsilon)\geq 2^{k}\cdot\left(1-O\left({(1+\nicefrac{{k^{2}}}{{d^{2}}})\varepsilon}\right)\right)\cdot\mathscr{E}\Big(\mathfrak{G},1-O\Big(\sqrt{(1+\nicefrac{{k^{2}}}{{d^{2}}})\varepsilon}\Big)\Big).

Proof of perfect completeness
(1)

Recall the notation
\mathcal{V}^{S^{\prime}}
for the restriction to
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{S^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{V}
with outcomes in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{S^{\prime}\sqcup S^{\prime\prime}}
(Definition
3.32
).

Assume
\mathfrak{G}
has a perfect
m
-dimensional
\mathsf{ZPC}
strategy
\mathscr{S}=\{\mathcal{U}\}
. Then, it induces an honest
\mathsf{ZPC}
strategy
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}=\{\mathcal{V}\}
which is perfect for
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
(Definition
4.4
and Claim
4.6
).
Let us extend
\mathcal{V}
to the other vertices so it becomes a perfect
\mathsf{ZPC}
strategy for
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
. First, let
\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}
be the appropriate restrictions of
\rho\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
, where
\rho
is the representation from Definition
3.68
, to the
X
and
Z
subgroups of
{\rm P}_{k}
, namely

\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)=\rho^{\mathds{X}}(\alpha)\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}={\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\quad,\quad\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(\alpha)=\rho^{\mathds{Z}}(\alpha)\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\ .

By Claim
3.82
, it can be extended to the rest of the
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
vertices in a
\mathsf{ZPC}
manner such that on the copy of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
in
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
it has value
1
.
As described in Corollary
3.39
, by calculating the inverse Fourier transform of the data processed
\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{\cdot}(\cdot)=\cdot]}
, and by denoting
\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{\cdot}
for the multiplication from the left of (the row vector)
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}
with the matrix
\mathfrak{s}^{\cdot}\in M_{r\times k}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
, we have

\forall\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}\ \colon\ \ \mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}(\alpha)={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\ ,\ \mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{B}}(\beta)={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{B}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\ \in\ U(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\ .

Note that in particular we have the following relationship through
\mathfrak{s}^{\cdot}
-evaluation (data processing, Definition
3.32
) in representation form

\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{A}]}\quad,\quad\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{B}]}\ .

As the rest of the checks in
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
are linear consistency checks, by Claim
3.36
, they are forcing us to choose the following PVMs (in representation form) to be the same —

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A}}\;,

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{B}}\ ,\

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{B}}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Read}\mathsf{Que}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{A}]}\;,

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Read}\mathsf{Que}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{B}]},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Perp}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}_{[(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}]}\;,

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Perp}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}_{[(\mathfrak{s}^{B})^{\perp}]}\ .

By choosing the above extension of
\mathcal{V}
, we are guaranteed that it passes all the augmented edges perfectly (both from the Sampling apparatus and the Hiding apparatus). We already described why
\mathcal{V}
passes the copies of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
and
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
perfectly, so, if it is well defined, then it is a perfect strategy for the game
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
.
Furthermore, it is straightforward to check that all the observables we chose are signed permutation matrices, and the readable ones are diagonal; hence, if this strategy is well defined, it is a perfect
Z
-aligned permutation strategy.

We are left to argue why
\mathcal{V}
is well defined — we chose restrictions of the PVMs at each vertex in a well defined manner, but it may be that these restricted PVMs do not amount to a single global one at the vertex, as they may be non-commuting. In addition, we need to check that
\mathcal{V}
is commuting along edges. These are all quite straightforward checks (or, are corollaries of Claims
3.82
and
4.6
), except for the
\mathtt{Read}_{\cdot}
-variables. — as the images of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{\cdot}}
are of the form
{\mathds{X}}^{\cdot}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
, they may not commute with the images of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadQue}^{\cdot,}}
which are of the form
{\mathds{Z}}^{\cdot}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
, and the images of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{\cdot}}
which are of the form
\sum\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}\otimes\mathscr{A}^{z}
. Let us demonstrate why they are commuting nonetheless — we focus on
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, but the proof for
\mathtt{Read}_{B}
is almost identical. For every
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}
and
\beta\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, we have

\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{Read}\mathsf{Que}}^{A}}(\alpha)={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\;,\quad\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}(\beta)={\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\ ,

(53)

and thus they commute if and only if
\langle\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A},\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}\rangle=0
, where we think of both as row vectors.
By recalling the notation
*
for transposition of matrices with coefficients in
\mathbb{F}_{2}
(Section
3.7
), and by the choice of
(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
having rows in
\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
, we have

\langle\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A},\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}\rangle=\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A}\cdot((\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp})^{*}\cdot\beta^{*}=0\ ,

as
\mathfrak{s}^{A}\cdot((\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp})^{*}
is the matrix whose columns are the
\mathfrak{s}^{A}
-evaluation of the rows of
(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
. Now, for every pair
\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}
, by (
51
) and the fact we chose
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}
, we have

\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\ .

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}(\beta)\cdot\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\cdot\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}(\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\left({\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}}\cdot\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}\cdot{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}}\right)\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z+\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z+\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z+\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp})}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\ ,

where the first and last equations are by definition, the second equation is due to
{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\gamma}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\gamma}=\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z+\gamma}
, and the third equation is since
\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
is a linear combination of rows of
(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
, which means it is in the kernel of
\mathfrak{s}^{A}
and thus satisfies
\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z+\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp})=\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)
for every
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.
∎

Proof of soundness
(2)
and entanglement lower bound
(3)

The proof idea is as follows: Given an almost perfect strategy of
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
, we are going to perturb it so that it passes perfectly all non-
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
edges. We are then going to show that the resulting strategy is honest (Definition
4.4
) when restricted to the copy of
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
, and thus has the same value as some strategy of
\mathfrak{G}
(in a much smaller dimension). As we did not perturb the strategy too much, its value did not change too much, and we can deduce the soundness and entanglement lower bound claims.

Let us move to the formal proof.
Assume
\mathscr{S}=\{\mathcal{U}\}
is an
N
-dimensional strategy in observable form for
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
with value
1-\varepsilon
. For notational simplicity, let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=(1+\nicefrac{{k^{2}}}{{d^{2}}})\varepsilon
.
All
O
-notations in this proof are universal constants, and in particular are independent of
k,\mathscr{B},\mathfrak{G}
or the strategy
\mathscr{S}
— genuinely universal. We repeatedly use Claim
3.22
, replacing previous bounds on expectations by the same bounds on the maxima (up to a constant factor that is absorbed into the
O
and
\approx
notations).

As the copy of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
is played with probability
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
when running
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
, the restriction of
\mathscr{S}
to the vertices of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
passes it with value of at least
1-4\varepsilon
.
Hence, by Claim
3.83
and Fact
3.73
, there is a
\nicefrac{{k^{2}\varepsilon}}{{d^{2}}}
-near bijection (Definition
3.4
)
\omega\colon\mathbb{C}^{N}\to\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}
for which the corner POVM
\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}\omega^{*}
is
\nicefrac{{k^{2}\varepsilon}}{{d^{2}}}
-close to
\rho^{\mathds{X}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
, and the corner POVM
\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}\omega^{*}
is
\nicefrac{{k^{2}\varepsilon}}{{d^{2}}}
-close to
\rho^{\mathds{Z}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
; namely, using Claim
3.22
, we have

\begin{split}\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \left\|\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)\omega^{*}-{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\right\|_{hs}^{2}\;,&\quad\left\|\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(\alpha)\omega^{*}-{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\right\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\nicefrac{{k^{2}\varepsilon}}{{d^{2}}})\;,\\
&\textrm{and}\\
1-\tau(\omega^{*}\omega)\;,&\quad 1-\tau(\omega\omega^{*})\leq O(\nicefrac{{k^{2}\varepsilon}}{{d^{2}}})\;.\end{split}

(54)

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=(1+\nicefrac{{k^{2}}}{{d^{2}}})\varepsilon
. So, the above quantities are all
O(\varepsilon^{\prime})
. Moreover, by orthonormalization (Fact
3.21
), the corner PVMs
\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}\omega^{*}
and
\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{B}}\omega^{*}
are
O(\varepsilon^{\prime})
-close to genuine representations that we denote by
\theta^{A}
and
\theta^{B}
; namely, using Claim
3.22
, we have

\displaystyle\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\ \colon\ \ \left\|\omega\left(\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\right)\omega^{*}-\theta^{A}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\right\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon^{\prime})\ ,

(55)

\displaystyle\forall\beta^{\mathfrak{R}},\beta^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\ \colon\ \ \left\|\omega\left(\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{B}}(\beta^{\mathfrak{R}},\beta^{\mathfrak{L}})\right)\omega^{*}-\theta^{B}(\beta^{\mathfrak{R}},\beta^{\mathfrak{L}})\right\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon^{\prime})\ .

(56)

The following few calculations aim to show that
\theta^{\cdot}
almost commutes with
{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
for every
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
and with
{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
for every
\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{\cdot}
.
As
\mathscr{S}
has value
1-\varepsilon
, and the augmented edges are sampled with probability of at least
\nicefrac{{1}}{{24}}
, we can deduce from the definition of inconsistency (Definition
3.10
) and the equivalence of inconsistency and distance for projective measurements (item 1 of Proposition
3.12
) various results:

1.

By the comparison along
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
, we can deduce that
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{A}]}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A}}
; namely, using Claim
3.22
, Claim
3.38
and the notation
\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A}
for the product from the left of the row vector
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}
with the
r\times k
matrix
\mathfrak{s}^{A}
,

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}\

\displaystyle\colon\ \ \|\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}(\alpha)-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}}(\alpha\cdot\mathfrak{s}^{A})\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ ,

(57)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\

\displaystyle\colon\ \ \|\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ .

(58)

2.

By the comparison along
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
, we can deduce that
\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}}
, and using Claim
3.22
this implies

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \|\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(\alpha)-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}}(\alpha)\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ .

(59)

3.

By the comparison
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, we can deduce that
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A}}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}
, and using Claim
3.22
,

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}\

\displaystyle\colon\ \ \|\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}(\alpha)-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A}}(\alpha)\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ ,

(60)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\

\displaystyle\colon\ \ \|\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ .

(61)

4.

By the comparison
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, we can deduce that
\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}
, and using Claim
3.22
,

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\

\displaystyle\colon\ \ \|\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}(\alpha)-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}(\alpha)\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ .

(62)

5.

By the comparison
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}
, we can deduce that
\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}\simeq_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}_{[(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}]}
, and using Claim
3.22
and Claim
3.38
,

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\

\displaystyle\colon\ \ \left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}(\alpha)-\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}}\left(\alpha\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}\right)\right\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon)\ .

(63)

Thus, using the notation
\square\approx_{\varepsilon}\heartsuit
whenever
\|\square-\heartsuit\|_{hs}^{2}\leq\varepsilon
(similar to the distance notation, Definition
3.10
), we have that

\begin{split}\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda},z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamZ}}(z)\\
&=\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamZ}}(z)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\ ,\end{split}

(64)

where both approximations use (
58
) and (
59
). Therefore,

\begin{split}\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda},z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \theta^{A}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\cdot{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}&\approx_{O(\varepsilon^{\prime})}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\omega^{*}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\omega^{*}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon^{\prime})}{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\cdot\theta^{A}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\ ,\end{split}

(65)

where the first and last approximations are by (
55
) and (
54
), and the middle one is by (
64
). For the second and fourth approximations, note that
1-\tau(\omega^{*}\omega),1-\tau(\omega\omega^{*})\leq O(\varepsilon)
from (
54
) and that
\|\square\heartsuit\|_{hs}\leq\|\square\|_{op}\|\heartsuit\|_{hs}
for square matrices, therefore using Claim
3.5
, we have

\begin{split}\|\omega\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})({\rm Id}-\omega^{*}\omega)\mathcal{U}_{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\omega^{*}\|_{hs}^{2}&\leq\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})({\rm Id}-\omega^{*}\omega)\mathcal{U}_{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\|_{hs}^{2}+4\varepsilon\\
&\leq\underbrace{\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\|_{op}^{2}}_{=1}\underbrace{\|{\rm Id}-\omega^{*}\omega\|_{hs}^{2}}_{=1-\tau(\omega^{*}\omega)}\underbrace{\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(z)\|_{op}^{2}}_{=1}+4\varepsilon\\
&\leq 5\varepsilon\ .\end{split}

(66)

As the rows of
(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
span the kernel of
\mathfrak{s}^{A}
, for every
\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
there is a
\beta\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
such that
\alpha=\beta\cdot(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}
. Hence,

\begin{split}\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\ \colon\ \ \mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}(\beta)\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}(\beta)\\
&=\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}(\beta)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}}(\beta)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\ ,\end{split}

(67)

where the first and last approximations use (
61
) and (
63
), and the middle ones use (
62
). Therefore,

\begin{split}\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda},\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}\ \colon\ \ \theta^{A}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\cdot{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}&\approx_{O(\varepsilon^{\prime})}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\omega^{*}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon)}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)\omega^{*}\omega\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\omega^{*}\\
&\approx_{O(\varepsilon^{\prime})}{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\cdot\theta^{A}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})\ ,\end{split}

(68)

where the first and last approximations are due to (
55
) and (
54
), the second and fourth are using (
66
), and the middle approximation is by (
67
).

All in all, we deduced that the images of
\theta^{A}
(and similarly for
\theta^{B}
) almost commute with all the
{\mathds{Z}}
-matrices and certain
{\mathds{X}}
-matrices. This is (essentially) the end of the proof, in a similar manner to that of the Pauli basis game, in which getting to an approximate relations situation allows one to apply a group stability result to finish the argument. Here we also need to analyze why commuting with these specific matrices completes the proof, but this is quite straightforward.

Claim 4.9
.

Let
\rho\colon G\to U(N)
be a (unitary) representation of a finite group
G
, and
\phi\colon A\to U(N)
be a representation of a finite abelian group. Assume

\forall g\in G,a\in A\ \colon\|\rho(g)\phi(a)-\phi(a)\rho(g)\|_{hs}^{2}\leq\varepsilon.

Then, there is another representation
\xi\colon A\to U(N)
, such that
\xi(a)\phi(g)=\phi(g)\xi(a)
for every
a\in A
and
g\in G
, and

\forall a\in A\ \colon\ \ \|\xi(a)-\phi(a)\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon).

Proof.

The proof is a combination of an averaging trick common in the study of property
(T)
groups (cf.
[
Ioa
,
dlS22b
]
), and a strict version of the Gowers–Hatami theorem due to Akhtiamov–Dogon
[
AD22
]
. It can also be deduced directly from orthonormalization (Fact
3.21
), but we show a different argument.

First, let
\tilde{\phi}(a)=\Expectation_{g\in G}[\rho(g)\phi(a)\rho(g)^{-1}]\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
.
By our assumption,
\|\tilde{\phi}(a)-\phi(a)\|_{hs}\leq\Expectation_{g\in G}\|\rho(g)\phi(a)\rho(g)^{-1}-\phi(a)\|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}.

Moreover,
\tilde{\phi}
commutes with
\rho
.
Now, denote by
\mathscr{M}
the commutant of
\rho(G)
, namely the collection of matrices that commute with all the
\rho
-images of
G
. Then,
{\rm Im}\tilde{\phi}
is in
\mathscr{M}
, and we can apply the rest of our arguments in this von-Neumann algebra. Note that

\begin{split}\|\tilde{\phi}(a)\tilde{\phi}^{*}(a)-{\rm Id}\|_{hs}&\leq\Expectation_{g,h\in G}[\|\rho(g)\phi(a)\rho(g)^{-1}\rho(h)\phi(a)^{-1}\rho(h)^{-1}-\underbrace{{\rm Id}}_{\rho(g)\rho(g)^{-1}\rho(h)\phi(a)\phi(a)^{-1}\rho(h)^{-1}}\|_{hs}]\\
&=\Expectation_{g,h\in G}[\|\phi(a)\rho(g^{-1}h)-\rho(g^{-1}h)\phi(a)\|_{hs}]\\
&\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

Then, by Lemma 2.2 in
[
AD22
]
, there is a map
\zeta\colon A\to U(\mathscr{M})
, namely to unitaries in the von-Neumann algebra
\mathscr{M}
such that
\|\zeta(a)-\tilde{\phi}(a)\|_{hs}\leq\|\tilde{\phi}(a)\tilde{\phi}^{*}(a)-{\rm Id}\|_{hs}
(this is quite straightforward from the SVD decomposition). Note in addition that

\begin{split}\forall a,b\in A\ \colon\ \ \|\zeta(a)\zeta(b)-\zeta(ab)\|_{hs}&\leq\|\zeta(a)-\tilde{\phi}(a)\|_{hs}+\|\zeta(b)-\tilde{\phi}(b)\|_{hs}\\
&+\|\zeta(ab)-\tilde{\phi}(ab)\|_{hs}+\|\tilde{\phi}(a)\tilde{\phi}(b)-\tilde{\phi}(ab)\|_{hs}\\
\forall a,b\in A\ \colon\ \ \|\tilde{\phi}(a)\tilde{\phi}(b)-\tilde{\phi}(ab)\|_{hs}&\leq\|\phi(a)-\tilde{\phi}(a)\|_{hs}+\|\phi(b)-\tilde{\phi}(b)\|_{hs}\\
&+\|\phi(ab)-\tilde{\phi}(ab)\|_{hs}+\|\phi(a)\phi(b)-\phi(ab)\|_{hs}\\
\end{split}

Now,
\phi(a)\phi(b)=\phi(ab)
since
\phi
is a representation, and all other summands are bounded by
\sqrt{\varepsilon}
. Hence,

\forall a,b\in A\ \colon\ \ \|\zeta(a)\zeta(b)-\zeta(ab)\|_{hs}\leq 6\sqrt{\varepsilon}.

By
[
AD22
, Corollary 1.7 and Claim 3.3]
, there is a unitary representation
\xi\colon A\to U(\mathscr{M})
55
55

55

Note that
\mathscr{M}
is the same! This is the main difference between Akhtiamov–Dogon to the standard Gowers–Hatami. This is possible thanks to the additional assumption that
A
is abelian.
such that

\forall a\in A\ \colon\ \ \|\xi(a)-\zeta(a)\|_{hs}\leq O(\sqrt{\varepsilon}).

Applying several triangle inequalities shows that

\forall a\in A\ \colon\ \ \|\xi(a)-\phi(a)\|_{hs}\leq O(\sqrt{\varepsilon}),

which in turn finishes the proof.
∎

By applying Claim
4.9
where
G
is the group generated by
{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
and
{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
for all
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k},\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
,
\rho
is the identity map, and with
A=\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}
and
\phi=\theta^{A}
, we deduce that there is a representation
\xi^{A}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\to U(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\xi^{A}
commutes with all
{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
and
{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
and also

\forall a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\ \colon\ \ \|\theta^{A}(a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})-\xi^{A}(a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})\|_{hs}^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon^{\prime})\;.

(69)

Moreover, everything can be done similarly for
B
resulting with a
\xi^{B}
that commutes with all
{\mathds{Z}}^{z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
and
{\mathds{X}}^{\beta}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
for every
\beta\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{B}

.

Now, we can define an
2^{k}\times m
-dimensional strategy
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}=\{\mathcal{V}\}
almost as we did in the completeness proof:

•

We let
\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}(\alpha)={\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m},\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}(\alpha)={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
,
and extend it to a perfect strategy of the Pauli basis game using Claim
3.82
.

•

For the other variables, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A}}=\xi^{A}\

\displaystyle,\ \mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadAns}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamAns}^{B}}=\xi^{B}\ ,\

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{SamZ}^{B}}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Read}\mathsf{Que}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{A}]}\

\displaystyle,\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Que}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Read}\mathsf{Que}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}}_{[\mathfrak{s}^{B}]},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Perp}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}_{[(\mathfrak{s}^{A})^{\perp}]}\

\displaystyle,\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Perp}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{B}}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}}_{[(\mathfrak{s}^{B})^{\perp}]}\ .

Now,
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}
is indeed a strategy — namely, all images are order
2
unitaries that commute for every fixed vertex — and it passes by design all checks in
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
with probability
1
, except for maybe
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}
. For that edge, we note that by (
69
), (
59
), (
57
), (
55
) and (
56
) the strategy
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}=\{\mathcal{V}\}
is
O(\varepsilon^{\prime})
-close on this edge to the original strategy
\{\mathcal{U}\}
, and Claim
3.29
states that this means they produce
\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
-close correlations, and thus
\mathcal{V}
passes this edge with probability close to that of
\mathcal{U}
. As this edge is sampled with probability
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
in
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
,
\mathcal{U}
passes it with probability
1-O(\varepsilon)
, which means
\mathcal{V}
passes it with probability
1-O(\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}})
.

We are left to show that
\mathcal{V}
is an honest strategy, and thus (by definition) a strategy with the same value can be extracted for
\mathfrak{G}
. This is immediate by analyzing the commutant of
\{{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m},{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}\}_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k},\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}}
. A matrix that commutes with all
{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes z}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
is of the form
\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}\otimes\mathscr{A}^{z}
for
\mathscr{A}^{z}\in M_{m}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}
the projections on the indicators
{\bf 1}_{z}
in
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}
(Definition
3.67
).
For such matrices, commuting with
{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\alpha}\otimes{\rm Id}_{m}
is the same as requiring
\mathscr{A}^{z}=\mathscr{A}^{z+\alpha}
for every
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.
Hence, the commutant consists of all matrices of the form
\sum_{z}(\sum_{\alpha\in\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z+\alpha})\otimes\mathscr{A}^{z}
where the sum over
z
’s takes a representative from every coset of
\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A}
in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
. But, this is the same as writing every matrix as
\sum_{\mathtt{x}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}}(\sum_{z\colon\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)=\mathtt{x}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z})\otimes\mathscr{A}^{\mathtt{x}}
. As this is true, in particular, for any projection in the commutant, we can write the PVM associated with the images of
\mathcal{V}
at
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}
as
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathtt{Intro}_{A}}_{\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}}}=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}:\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)=\mathtt{x}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{z}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\mathtt{x}}_{a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}}}
, and similarly for
\mathtt{Intro}_{B}
. The resulting
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\to M_{m}(\mathbb{C})
is a PVM strategy for
\mathfrak{G}
that passes it with the same probability as
\mathcal{V}
passes
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}
(as in Definition
4.4
on honest strategies), which is
1-O(\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}})
. This finishes the proof of soundness.

Note that by (
54
), the normalized dimension difference
1-\frac{N}{2^{k}\cdot m}\leq 1-\tau(\omega\omega^{*})\leq O(\varepsilon^{\prime}).

Furthermore, as we extracted from the honest
\mathcal{V}
an
m
-dimensional strategy

\mathcal{P}
for
\mathfrak{G}
with value
1-O(\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}})
, we deduce that
m\geq\mathscr{E}(\mathfrak{G},1-O(\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}))
, which proves the entanglement lower bound
(3)
.

4.3
Conditionally linear maps

z^{V_{1}}
z^{V_{>1}}
\mathtt{x}_{1}=\mathfrak{s}_{1}(z^{V_{1}})
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
z^{V_{2}}
z^{V_{>2}}
\mathtt{x}_{2}=\mathfrak{s}_{2}(z^{V_{2}})
\mathfrak{s}_{2}=\mathfrak{s}_{2}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}
\;\cdots\cdots

Figure 11:
An illustration of an
h
-level CLM
\mathfrak{s}
(adapted from
[
JNV+21
, Figure 1]
). Let
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
be the input.
First a register subspace
V_{1}
and a linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
are chosen and
applied on the restriction of
z
to
V_{1}
to obtain
\mathtt{x}_{1}=\mathfrak{s}_{1}(z^{V_{1}})\in V_{1}
.
Then depending the value of
\mathtt{x}_{1}
, a register subspace
V_{2}=V_{2}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}
and a
linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{2}=\mathfrak{s}_{2}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}
are chosen and applied on the restriction of
z
to
V_{2}
to obtain

\mathtt{x}_{2}=\mathfrak{s}_{2}(z^{V_{2}})\in V_{2}
and so on.
Finally,
\mathfrak{s}(z)
is defined to be

\sum_{j=1}^{h}\mathtt{x}_{j}
.

The collection of linear maps
\mathfrak{s}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}
induces a family of samplers which is too restrictive for us to prove compression with. But, a certain generalization of linear maps, called
conditionally linear maps

[
JNV+21
, Definition 4.1]
, are rich enough to deduce compression. This section is devoted to this generalized setup.

Intuitively, conditionally linear maps that act on
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
apply a sequence of linear maps on subspaces of it, where each map in the sequence depends on the value which the previous linear maps produced. These maps are in a sweet spot, being rich enough so that all the samplers that we will need can be described as pushforwards of the uniform measure along them, while being amenable to a construction similar to
\mathfrak{Baby}(\mathfrak{G})
from Section
4.2
.

Conditionally linear maps are not complicated objects, yet the notation associated with them can take some time getting used to. We recommend that the reader attempt to follow the visual explanation given in Figure
11
first, to form their own intuition; which can then be matched to the formal definitions that follow.

Definition 4.10
.

A
register subspace
of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
is one which is spanned by some subset of the standard basis
\{e_{1},...,e_{k}\}
. As there is a bijection between register subspaces and subsets of
[k]
, we often associate with such a subspace the appropriate subset of indices
I\subseteq[k]
. Given a register subspace
V=\mathrm{Span}\{e_{i_{1}},...,e_{i_{m}}\}
(in which case
I=\{i_{1},...,i_{m}\}
) and a vector
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
we denote by
z^{V}
the restriction of
z
to the coordinates of
V
, namely
z^{V}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\langle z,e_{i_{j}}\rangle e_{i_{j}}
. As
V
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{I}
, we often treat vectors in
V
as parameterized by
I
instead of
[k]
.

Two register subspaces are said to be
disjoint
if their intersection is trivial. Two register subspaces are said to be
complementary
if they are disjoint and sum up to the whole space
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.

Definition 4.11

(Conditionally linear map — recursive definition)
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
h\geq 0
be integers. The collection of
h
-level conditionally linear maps (CLMs) on
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
is defined inductively on
h
as follows.

•

A
0
-level CLM over
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
is the zero map.

•

Assume
(h-1)
-level CLMs were already defined. An
h
-level CLM
\mathfrak{s}
over
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
consists of the following data:

–

a register subspace (Definition
4.10
)
V_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, whose complement is denoted by
V_{>1}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
;

–

a linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\colon V_{1}\to V_{1}
;

–

for every
u\in V_{1}
, an
(h-1)
-level CLM
\mathfrak{s}_{>1}^{u}
on
V_{>1}
.

The data of an
h
-level CLM defines a function
\mathfrak{s}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
as follows:

•

For the
0
-level case the function is the zero function.

•

Assuming we defined evaluation along
(h-1)
-level CLMs, the evaluation along an
h
-level CLM is:

–

Given
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, recall that
z^{V_{1}}\in V_{1}
is its restriction to
V_{1}
. One can thus use the linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\colon V_{1}\to V_{1}
to evaluate
\mathtt{x}_{1}=\mathfrak{s}_{1}(z^{V_{1}})
.

–

As there is an
(h-1)
-level CLM associated to
\mathtt{x}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{s}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}_{>1}\colon V_{>1}\to V_{>1}
, and as we assumed evaluation along
(h-1)
-level CLMs was already defined, we let
\mathtt{x}_{>1}=\mathfrak{s}_{>1}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}(z^{V_{>1}})\in V_{>1}
, where again
z^{V_{>1}}
is the restriction of the input
z
to the complementary subspace
V_{>1}
.

–

Finally, the
\mathfrak{s}
-evaluation of
z
is defined to be
\mathfrak{s}(z)=\mathtt{x}_{1}+\mathtt{x}_{>1}\in V_{1}\oplus V_{>1}=\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.

Remark 4.12
.

Note that
1
-level CLMs are exactly linear functions from
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
to itself. An example of a
2
-level CLM is
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\mapsto(0,x_{1}x_{3}+x_{2}x_{3}+x_{1},x_{3})
— this example can be used as a sanity check, and a proof of it being a
2
-level CLM appears in
[
JNV+21
, Example 4.3]
.

The following is a more intricate definition of
h
-level CLMs, which avoids the recursive nature of Definition
4.11
. A proof that the two definitions are equivalent is given in
[
JNV+21
, Lemma 4.6]
(with somewhat different notation).

Definition 4.13

(Conditionally linear map — direct definition)
.

Let
h\geq 0
and
k\geq 1
be integer. To describe an
h
-level conditionally linear map
(CLM)
\mathfrak{s}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
we need the following structure.
First, there is a collection of register subspaces defined inductively:

•

V_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
is a fixed register subspace, and we denote by
V_{>1}
its complement.

•

For every
u_{1}\in V_{1}
, there is a register subspace
V_{2}^{u_{1}}\subseteq V_{>1}
, and we denote the sum
V_{1}\oplus V^{u_{1}}_{2}
by
V_{\leq 2}^{u_{1}}
, and its complement by
V_{>2}^{u_{1}}
.

•

Then, for every
u_{2}\in V_{2}^{u_{1}}
, there is a register subspace
V_{3}^{u_{1},u_{2}}\subseteq V^{u_{1}}_{>2}
, which gives rise to the subspaces
V_{\leq 3}^{u_{1},u_{2}}=V^{u_{1}}_{\leq 2}\oplus V^{u_{1},u_{2}}_{3}
and its complement
V_{>3}^{u_{1},u_{2}}
.

•

This keeps on, so that in the
j^{\rm th}
step, for every
u_{1}\in V_{1},u_{2}\in V_{2}^{u_{1}},\dots,u_{j-1}\in V_{j-1}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-2}}
there is a register subspace
V_{j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}
disjoint of
V_{1}\oplus V_{2}^{u_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus V_{j-1}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-2}}
, giving rise to the appropriate
V_{\leq j}=V_{\leq j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}
and complement
V_{>j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}
.

•

No matter the process, we are guaranteed to reach
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
after
h
steps, namely
V_{1}\oplus V_{2}^{u_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus V_{h}^{u_{1},...,u_{h-1}}=\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
for every
u_{1}\in V_{1},u_{2}\in V_{2}^{u_{1}},\dots,u_{h-1}\in V_{h-1}^{u_{1},...,u_{h-1}}
.

Now, in addition to the above collections of register subspaces, there is a collection of linear maps on them:

•

On
V_{1}
there is a fixed
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\colon V_{1}\to V_{1}
.

•

For every
u_{1}\in V_{1}
there is a linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{2}^{u_{1}}\colon V_{2}^{u_{1}}\to V_{2}^{u_{1}}
.

•

This continues in a similar manner to before, where in the
j^{\rm th}
step, for every
u_{1}\in V_{1},u_{2}\in V_{2}^{u_{1}},\dots,u_{j-1}\in V_{j-1}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-2}}
there is a linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{j}=\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}\colon V_{j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}\to V_{j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}
.

Finally, the function
\mathfrak{s}
is calculated as follows:

•

Let
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
be the input to
\mathfrak{s}
.

•

Calculate
\mathtt{x}_{1}=\mathfrak{s}_{1}(z^{V_{1}})
, and let
V_{2}=V_{2}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}
.

•

Calculate
\mathtt{x}_{2}=\mathfrak{s}_{2}^{\mathtt{x}_{1}}(z^{V_{2}})
, and let
V_{3}=V_{3}^{\mathtt{x}_{1},\mathtt{x}_{2}}
.

•

In the
j^{\rm th}
step, calculate
\mathtt{x}_{j}=\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{\mathtt{x}_{1},...,\mathtt{x}_{j-1}}(z^{V_{j}})
, and let
V_{j+1}=V_{j+1}^{\mathtt{x}_{1},...,\mathtt{x}_{j}}
.

•

After
h
steps are completed, resulting in
\mathtt{x}_{1},...,\mathtt{x}_{h}
, output
\mathfrak{s}(z)=\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{1}+\mathtt{x}_{2}+...+\mathtt{x}_{h}\in V_{1}\oplus\dots\oplus V_{h}=\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{s}_{j}(z)
the value of
\mathtt{x}_{j}
in the above computation of
\mathfrak{s}(z)
, and let

\mathfrak{s}_{\leq j}(z)=\mathtt{x}_{\leq j}=\mathtt{x}_{1}+...+\mathtt{x}_{j}

(70)

be the cumulative output up to the
j^{\rm th}
computation.

Definition 4.14

(Seeded conditionally linear maps)
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be an
h
-level CLM with all the data from Definition
4.13
. Let
u\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
be a vector which plays the role of a
seed
. Then,
u
induces a decomposition of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
into
h
disjoint register subspaces, which we call the
u
-seeded register subspaces
, as follows:

•

Regardless of
u
, we let
W_{1}^{u}=V_{1}
. As
W_{1}^{u}
is a register subspace, it has an associated subset of indices from
[k]
, which we denote by
I^{u}_{1}\subseteq[k]
. Furthermore, denote the restriction of
u
to this register subspace by
u_{1}=u^{W_{1}^{u}}
.

•

Then, we let
W_{2}^{u}=V_{2}^{u_{1}}
with
I^{u}_{2}\subseteq[k]
the associated subset of indices, and denote the restriction of
u
to it by
u_{2}=u^{W^{u}_{2}}
.

•

More generally, given that we have already defined
W^{u}_{1},...,W^{u}_{j-1}
and thus the respective restrictions
u_{1},...,u_{j-1}
of
u
, we let

W^{u}_{j}=V_{j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}\quad\textrm{and}\quad u_{j}=u^{W^{u}_{j}}\ ,

(71)

with
I^{u}_{j}\subseteq[k]
being again the associated subset of indices.

We use
W_{\leq j}^{u},W_{<j}^{u},W_{\geq j}^{u}
and
W_{>j}^{u}
in a similar way to before, and
I^{u}_{\leq j},I^{u}_{<j},I^{u}_{\geq j},I^{u}_{>j}
for the indices supporting each of these register subspaces. We call

u_{\leq j}=u_{1}+...+u_{j}\in W^{u}_{1}\oplus...\oplus W^{u}_{j}

(72)

the
j^{\rm th}

prefix of

u
, and note that
W^{u}_{\leq j+1}
,
W^{u}_{>j+1}
depend only on this
j^{\rm th}
prefix and not all of
u
. Note also that
\mathfrak{s}_{\leq j}(z)
as in (
70
) is
equal
to the
j^{\rm th}
prefix of
\mathfrak{s}(z)
, namely to
(\mathfrak{s}(z))_{\leq j}
, since
\mathfrak{s}
uses its partial computation as the seed to the rest of it. We also have the notions of
u
-seeded
j^{\rm th}
-position, prefix and suffix
of any vector, which are defined by

\forall z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ z^{u}_{j}=z^{W^{u}_{j}}\;,\quad z^{u}_{\leq j}=z^{W^{u}_{\leq j}}\;,\quad z^{u}_{\geq j}=z^{W^{u}_{\geq j}}\ .

(73)

The
u
-seeded CLM
, denoted by
\mathfrak{s}^{u}
, is the following linear map: Letting
u_{i}=u^{W^{u}_{i}}
be as in (
71
), we define the
j^{\rm th}

u
-seeded linear map
\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{u}\colon W^{u}_{j}\to W^{u}_{j}
by

\forall z\in W^{u}_{j}\ \colon\ \ \mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}(z)=\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{u_{1},...,u_{j-1}}(z)\ .

(74)

Namely,
\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{u}
uses the
(j-1)^{\rm th}
prefix of
u
as a seed, which defines both the appropriate
j^{\rm th}
subspace
W^{u}_{j}
(and thus the restriction of
z
to this subspace) as well as the
j^{\rm th}
linear map
\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}=\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{u_{<j}}
acting on this subspace. Then, we let
\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{\leq j}\colon W^{u}_{\leq j}\to W^{u}_{\leq j}
be

\mathfrak{s}_{\leq j}^{u}=\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{2}\oplus...\oplus\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathfrak{s}^{u}=\mathfrak{s}_{\leq h}^{u}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ .

(75)

As
\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}\colon W^{u}_{j}\to W^{u}_{j}
are linear maps, and
I^{u}_{j}
is the set of indices associated with
W^{u}_{j}
, we think of them as matrices represented in the standard basis supported on
I^{u}_{j}
, i.e., for every
i,t\in I^{u}_{j}
we have
(\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j})_{it}=\langle e_{i},\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}(e_{t})\rangle
.

We often use the following natural extension of the
j^{\rm th}

u
-seeded linear map to all of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
: As
W^{u}_{j}
has a complement register subspace
W^{u}_{\neq j}
, which is the sum of all
W^{u}_{i}
such that
i\neq j
, we can let the
extended
j^{\rm th}

u
-seeded linear map

\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{j}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
by letting

\forall z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \mathfrak{S}^{u}_{j}(z)=\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}(z^{u}_{j})\ ,

(76)

namely, it acts the same way as
\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j}
on
W^{u}_{j}
, and sends everything else to zero. We use a similar notation as before,
\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{\leq j}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{i}
, and note that
\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{\leq h}=\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{\leq h}=\mathfrak{s}^{u}
.

Corollary 4.15

(Seeded versus unseeded CLMs)
.

Let
h
and
k
be positive integers, and let
\mathfrak{s}
be an
h
-level CLM on
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
. Let
j\in[h]
,
u\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
a seed and
z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
a vector. Then,
\mathfrak{s}_{\leq j}(z)=u_{\leq j}
as defined in (
70
) if and only if
\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{\leq j}(z^{u}_{\leq j})=u_{\leq j}
as defined in (
75
). In particular,
\mathfrak{s}(z)=u
if and only if
\mathfrak{s}^{u}(z)=u
.

Definition 4.16

(Sampling scheme induced by
h
-level CLMs)
.

A tailored game
\mathfrak{G}
is said to have a sampling scheme induced by
h
-level CLMs, if there exist a pair of
h
-level CLMs
\mathfrak{s}=(\mathfrak{s}^{A},\mathfrak{s}^{B})\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
(Definition
4.13
), such that the vertex set of the underlying graph of
\mathfrak{G}
is
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, and the distribution over edges is the pushforward of the uniform distribution over
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
through
\mathfrak{s}
. Namely, for
\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
,

\mu(\mathtt{x}\mathtt{y})=\frac{|\{z\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\mid\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z)=\mathtt{x},\mathfrak{s}^{B}(z)=\mathtt{y}\}|}{2^{k}}\ .

Perpendicular maps

As seen in the baby question reduction transformation from Section
4.2
, we need a notion of a “perpendicular map”. Specifically for the full question reduction transformation, we will need a perpendicular map for every seeded CLM (Definition
4.14
).

Definition 4.17
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
be a linear map. A
perpendicular map
to
f
, is a linear map
f^{\perp}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
whose rows span
\ker(f)
, namely
{\rm Im}((f^{\perp})^{*})=\ker(f)
, where
*
is the dual map with respect to the bilinear form
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
— see the beginning of Section
3.7
.

Remark 4.18
.

As defined, the perpendicular map is not unique. There is an efficient algorithmic way of extracting a perpendicular map given the matrix representation of a linear map using Gaussian elimination — see, e.g.,
[
JNV+21
, Definition 3.11]
.

Claim 4.19
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
be a linear map, and let
f^{\perp}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
be a perpendicular map to
f
(Definition
4.17
), namely a matrix whose rows span the kernel of
f
. Then, the
f
-evaluated (Definition
3.32
) PVM
\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}
commutes with the
f^{\perp}
-evaluated PVM
\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{X}}
. Namely,

\forall\nu,\mathtt{x}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\ \colon\ \ \mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{[f(\cdot)=\mathtt{x}]}\cdot\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{X}}_{[f^{\perp}(\cdot)=\nu]}=\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{X}}_{[f^{\perp}(\cdot)=\nu]}\cdot\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}_{[f(\cdot)=\mathtt{x}]}\ .

In words, one can measure the
f
-evaluation according to the
{\mathds{Z}}
-basis simultaneously with the
f^{\perp}
-evaluation according to the
{\mathds{X}}
-basis.

Proof.

These two PVMs commute in projective form if and only if they commute in representation form. Let
\rho^{\mathds{Z}}
and
\rho^{\mathds{X}}
be the representation forms of
\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{Z}}
and
\mathscr{F}^{\mathds{X}}
respectively (as they were defined in (
46
)), namely
\rho^{\mathds{Z}}(\alpha)={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha}
and
\rho^{\mathds{X}}(\beta)={\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta}
. Then, by Corollary
3.39
,

\rho^{\mathds{Z}}_{[f]}(\alpha)={\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha\cdot f}\quad,\quad\rho^{\mathds{X}}_{[f^{\perp}]}(\beta)={\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot f^{\perp}}\ .

Now, for every
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, thought of as row vectors, we have

{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha\cdot f}{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot f^{\perp}}=(-1)^{\langle\alpha\cdot f,\beta\cdot f^{\perp}\rangle}{\mathds{X}}^{\otimes\beta\cdot f^{\perp}}{\mathds{Z}}^{\otimes\alpha\cdot f}\ .

But,

\langle\alpha\cdot f,\beta\cdot f^{\perp}\rangle=\alpha\underbrace{f(f^{\perp})^{*}}_{=0}\beta^{*}=0\ ,

where
*
is transposition in the above calculation. Hence the PVMs commute as claimed.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

In the next section, we assume to be given in addition to a CLM
\mathfrak{s}
acting on
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, a collection of perpendicular maps
(\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{u})^{\perp}\colon W^{u}_{j}\to W^{u}_{j}
for each
j^{\rm th}

u
-seeded CLM. In the same spirit as before, we use the notation
(\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{\leq j})^{\perp}
for
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}(\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{i})^{\perp}
, and it is indeed perpendicular to the map
\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{\leq j}
.

By extending
(\mathfrak{s}^{u}_{j})^{\perp}
to
(\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{j})^{\perp}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
to be the identity on
W^{u}_{\neq j}
we indeed obtain a function which is perpendicular to the extended
j^{\rm th}

u
-seeded CLM defined in (
76
) — so the notation is fitting. This extension satisfies that

(\mathfrak{S}_{\leq j}^{u})^{\perp}=(\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{j})^{\perp}\circ...\circ(\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{2})^{\perp}\circ(\mathfrak{S}^{u}_{1})^{\perp}\ .

(77)

4.4
Question Reduction in the conditionally linear sampler case

Note
, this is the proper augmentation that is used in compression, as opposed to the simplified case described and analyzed in Section
4.2
. The sections are structured in a very similar manner, in the hope that by first reading Section
4.2
, the following description and analysis of the proper augmentation become clear.

Let
\mathfrak{G}
be a tailored game with the following properties:

(1)

Its sampling scheme is induced by
h
-level CLMs (Definition
4.16
), for some positive integer
h
. Namely, its vertex set is
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, and the distribution on edges is induced by the pushforward of the uniform distribution on
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
through a pair of
h
-level CLMs (Definition
4.13
)
\mathfrak{s}=(\mathfrak{s}^{A},\mathfrak{s}^{B})\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
.

(2)

Its length functions are constant and equal to some positive integer
\Lambda
.

(3)

In a similar manner to Section
4.2
, we need a basis for the kernel of the
j^{\rm th}

u
-seeded CLM
\mathfrak{s}^{A,u}_{j}\colon W^{A,u}_{j}\to W^{A,u}_{j}
(Definition
4.14
), which is a linear map, for every
j
and
u
. So, we assume to be given perpendicular maps (Definition
4.17
)
(\mathfrak{s}^{A,u}_{j})^{\perp}\colon W^{A,u}_{j}\to W^{A,u}_{j}
, namely their rows, parametrized by the indices in
I^{A,u}_{j}\subset[k]
, span
\ker\mathfrak{s}^{A,u}_{j}
. We mainly use the extensions
(\mathfrak{S}^{A,u}_{j})^{\perp}
of these maps to all of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
as in Remark
4.20
, and specifically those defined in (
77
).

Let
\mathfrak{G}
be a tailored game with vertex set
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
and whose distribution on edges is induced by the pushforward of the uniform distribution on
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
through a pair of
h
-level CLMs
\mathfrak{s}=(\mathfrak{s}^{A},\mathfrak{s}^{B})\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
. As usual, it is assumed that
\mathfrak{G}
has constant readable and unreadable answer lengths both equal to
\Lambda
. In addition, a collection of perpendicular maps
(\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{A,\mathtt{x}})^{\perp}
to the seeded CLMs
\mathfrak{s}_{j}^{A,\mathtt{x}}
are assumed to be provided, and we use the notation
(\mathfrak{S}_{j}^{A,\mathtt{x}})^{\perp}
for their extensions as in Remark
4.20
.

The following tests are performed when the corresponding augmented edge is sampled:

1.

\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{\cdot}
: Check that
z=z_{sam}
.

2.

\mathtt{Intro}_{\cdot}-\mathtt{Sample}_{\cdot}
: Check that
\mathtt{x}=\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z_{sam})
,
a^{\mathfrak{R}}=a^{\mathfrak{R}}_{sam}
and
a^{\mathfrak{L}}=a^{\mathfrak{L}}_{sam}
.

3.

\mathtt{Intro}_{\cdot}-\mathtt{Read}_{\cdot}
: Check that
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{read}
,
a^{\mathfrak{R}}=a^{\mathfrak{R}}_{read}
and
a^{\mathfrak{L}}=a^{\mathfrak{L}}_{read}
.

4.

\mathtt{Hide}_{\cdot}^{h}-\mathtt{Read}_{\cdot}
: Check that
\nu_{hide\ h}=\nu_{read}
and that
\mathtt{x}_{hide\ h}=(\mathtt{x}_{read})_{<h}
, where
(\cdot)_{<h}
is the
(h-1)^{\rm th}
prefix (
72
).

5.

\mathtt{Hide}_{\cdot}^{1}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
: Check that
\mathtt{x}_{hide\ 1}=0
and that
\nu_{hide\ 1}=(\mathfrak{S}_{1}^{A,\mathtt{x}_{hide\ 1}})^{\perp}(\chi)
.

6.

\mathtt{Hide}_{\cdot}^{j}-\mathtt{Hide}_{\cdot}^{j-1}
: Fixing
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j}
, we check two things. First, that
\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j-1}=\mathtt{x}_{<j-1}
, where
(\cdot)_{<j-1}
is the
(j-2)^{\rm th}
prefix (
72
). Second, that
(\mathfrak{S}^{A,\mathtt{x}}_{j})^{\perp}(\nu_{hide\ j-1})=\nu_{hide\ j}
.

Figure 12:
Questions and answers in the game
\mathfrak{QueRed}_{h}(\mathfrak{G},k,\mathscr{B})
. Since the game is an augmentation of the sum of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}(\mathscr{B})
and
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
we only list new questions and answers, and additional tests, and refer to Figure
3
and Figure

for questions and answers of the latter.

The question reduction transformation
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})=\mathfrak{QueRed}_{h}(\mathfrak{G},k,\mathscr{B})
(See Figure
12
for an overview): The inputs are expected to be a positive integer
k
, a tuple
\mathscr{B}
of
n
vectors in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
that induce and
[n,k,d]
-code, and a tailored game
\mathfrak{G}
satisfying
(1)
,
(2)
and
(3)
from the beginning of the section.
The game
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
is then an augmented (Definition
3.45
) sum (Definition
3.44
) of the Pauli basis game
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}={\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}(\mathscr{B})
(Section
3.8.3
) and the introspection game
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
(Definition
4.2
). The augmentation consists of two apparatuses:

1.

A
Sampling apparatus
which connects the introspection game vertices to the total
Z
-measurement of the Pauli basis game (i.e., the vertex
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}
). The goal of this apparatus is two-fold — first, to verify that the “questions” part of the players’ answers when the copy of
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
is played is distributed according to the question distribution of
\mathfrak{G}
— namely the pushforward of the uniform distribution along the CLMs
(\mathfrak{s}^{A},\mathfrak{s}^{B})
; second, to verify that the observables associated with the “answers“ part of the players’ answers in
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
commute with the total
Z
-measurement.

2.

A
Hiding apparatus
which connects the introspection game vertices to the total
X
-measurement of the Pauli basis game (i.e., the vertex
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}
). The goal of this apparatus is to verify that the “answers“ part of the players’ answers in
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
commute with a certain data processing of the
X
-measurements, specifically through the map
(\mathfrak{s}^{\cdot,\cdot})^{\perp}
.

For the sampling apparatus, two vertices
\mathtt{Sample}_{A},\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
are added and are connected as follows

\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}\ .

For the hiding apparatus,
2h+2
vertices are added —
\mathtt{Read}_{A},\mathtt{Read}_{B},
and for every
1\leq j\leq h
the vertices
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j},\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{j}
— and are connected as follows

\begin{split}\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}-\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{h}-...-\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{1}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}-\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{1}-...-\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{h}-\mathtt{Read}_{B}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}\ .\end{split}

(78)

See Figure
13
for a graphical view of the underlying graph of
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
.

Question distribution of the question reduced game
:
56
56

56

The distribution that we eventually use is slightly different, as the sampler of this game needs to be induced by CLMs so that we can iterate compression. This is handled in Section
4.5.2
, where we provide a distribution such that for every edge the probability is the same as this one up to some global constant factor independent of
k,\mathscr{B}
or
\mathfrak{G}
, though it may depend on
h
.

With probability
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
do one of the following —

•

Sample an edge from
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
according to the appropriate distribution therein.

•

Sample the single edge
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}
from
\mathfrak{Intro}(\mathfrak{G})
.

•

Sample a uniformly random edge from the Sampling apparatus.

•

Sample a uniformly random edge from the Hiding apparatus.

The underlying graph of
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
, where most of the embedded Pauli basis game is hidden. Also, there are
h-2
extra vertices between
\mathtt{Hide}_{\cdot}^{1}
and

.

Lengths and formal generating sets for the augmented vertices of question reduction
(which are almost the same as in Section
4.2
):

Sampling apparatus
— The readable length of
\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
(and
\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
) is
k+\Lambda
, and its unreadable length is
\Lambda
. We associate with it the formal generators

\begin{split}S^{\mathfrak{R}}_{\mathtt{Sample}_{A}}&=\mathsf{SamZ}^{A}\sqcup\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R}}=\{\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,i},\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R},j}\mid 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\},\\
S^{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathtt{Sample}_{A}}&=\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}=\{\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L},j}\mid 1\leq j\leq\Lambda\}.\end{split}

and similarly for
\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
. Namely, answers are formatted as
(z_{sam},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}})
, where
z_{sam}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
, and
a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}
.

Hiding apparatus
—

•

The readable length of
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Read}_{B}
) is
k+\Lambda
, and its unreadable length is
k+\Lambda
as well. We associate with it the formal generators

\begin{split}S^{\mathfrak{R}}_{\mathtt{Read}_{A}}&=\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A}\sqcup\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R}}=\{\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A,i},\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{R},j}\mid 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\},\\
S^{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathtt{Read}_{A}}&=\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A}\sqcup\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}=\{\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A,i},\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\mathfrak{L},j}\mid 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\},\end{split}

and similarly for
\mathtt{Read}_{B}
. Namely, answers are formatted as
(\mathtt{x}_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\nu_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
, where
\mathtt{x}_{read}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}
and
\nu_{read}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
(and for
B
,
(\mathtt{y}_{read},b_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\mu_{read},b_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
in the appropriate spaces).

•

The readable length of
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{j}
) is
k
, and its unreadable length is
k
. We associate with it the formal generators

\begin{split}S^{\mathfrak{R}}_{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j}}&=\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Que}^{A}=\{\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k\},\\
S^{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j}}&=\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Perp}^{A}=\{\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k\},\end{split}

and similarly for
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{j}
. Namely, the answer is formatted as
(\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j},\nu_{hide\ j})\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2k}
(respectively

(\mathtt{y}_{hide\ j},\mu_{hide\ j})\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2k}
).

Decision procedure of the augmented edges in the question reduced game
: This is essentially the description of the controlled linear constraints function
L_{\mathtt{x}\mathtt{y}}
of
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
, but we use the phrase “check that” repeatedly, by which we mean “add this sequence of linear constraints to the image of
L_{\mathtt{x}\mathtt{y}}
and the canonical verifier will check them”.

Sampling apparatus
—

(1)

In case
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
) is sampled, check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq k\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{P}\mathsf{Z}^{i})=\gamma(\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,i})\ .

(79)

In other words, if
z
is the answer to
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}
, then check that
z=z_{sam}
.

(2)

In case
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Intro}_{B}-\mathtt{Sample}_{B}
) is sampled, first check that

\forall 1\leq j\leq\Lambda\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\cdot,j})=\gamma(\mathsf{SamAns}^{A,\cdot,j})

(80)

and then check that

\mathfrak{s}^{A}(\gamma(\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,1}),...,\gamma(\mathsf{SamZ}^{A,k}))=(\gamma(\mathsf{Que}^{A,1}),...,\gamma(\mathsf{Que}^{A,k}))\ .

(81)

In other words, if
(z_{sam},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}},a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Sample}_{A}
, and
(\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}
, then check that
\mathtt{x}=\mathfrak{s}^{A}(z_{sam}),a^{\mathfrak{R}}=a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{R}}
and
a^{\mathfrak{L}}=a_{sam}^{\mathfrak{L}}
.

Note that the check (
81
) is not linear (because
\mathfrak{s}^{A}
is only
conditionally
linear, not linear). But, as both
\mathsf{SamZ}^{\cdot,\cdot}
and
\mathsf{Que}^{\cdot,\cdot}
are readable variables, it can be tailored as follows:
L_{\mathtt{Intro}_{A}\ \mathtt{Sample}_{A}}(\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},z_{sam},a^{\mathfrak{R}}_{sam})
contains all constraints induced by (
80
), adds no additional linear constraints if (
81
) is satisfied, and adds
\{\mathsf{J}\}
as a constraint if (
81
) is not satisfied (which translates to definite rejection).

Hiding apparatus
—

(1)

In case
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Intro}_{B}-\mathtt{Read}_{B}
) is sampled, check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq\Lambda\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\cdot,j})=\gamma(\mathsf{ReadAns}^{A,\cdot,j})\;,\quad\gamma(\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})=\gamma(\mathsf{ReadQue}^{A,i})\ .

(82)

In other words, if
(\mathtt{x}_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\nu_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, and
(\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}
, check that
\mathtt{x}_{read}=\mathtt{x}
,
a^{\mathfrak{R}}_{read}=a^{\mathfrak{R}}
and
a^{\mathfrak{L}}_{read}=a^{\mathfrak{L}}
.

(2)

In case
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{h}-\mathtt{Read}_{A}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{h}-\mathtt{Read}_{B}
) is sampled: First check that

\forall 1\leq i\leq k\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{h}\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i})=\gamma(\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{A,i})\ .

(83)

Namely, if we denote by
(\mathtt{x}_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{R}},\nu_{read},a_{read}^{\mathfrak{L}})
the answer to
\mathtt{Read}_{A}
, and by
(\mathtt{x}_{hide\ h},\nu_{hide\ h})
the answer to
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{h}
, (
83
) checks that
\nu_{hide\ h}=\nu_{read}
. In addition, check that
\mathtt{x}_{hide\ h}
is the
(h-1)
-prefix (
72
) of
\mathtt{x}_{read}
, namely that
(\mathtt{x}_{read})_{\leq h-1}=\mathtt{x}_{hide\ h}
, or equivalently

\begin{split}\forall i\in I^{\mathtt{x}_{read}}_{<h}\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{h}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})&=\gamma(\mathsf{Read}^{h}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})\ ,\\
\forall i\in I^{\mathtt{x}_{read}}_{h}\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{h}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})&=0\ .\end{split}

(84)

Note that although these are linear checks, they depend on the value of
\mathtt{x}_{read}
. But, as
\mathsf{ReadQue}^{\cdot,\cdot}
are readable, this is allowed in the tailored category.

(3)

In case
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{1}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{1}-\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
) is sampled, let
\mathtt{x}:=\mathtt{x}_{hide\ 1}=(\gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{1}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i}))_{i=1}^{k}
, and check that

(\mathfrak{S}^{A,\mathtt{x}}_{1})^{\perp}(\gamma(\mathsf{P}\mathsf{X}^{i}))_{i=1}^{k}=(\gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{1}\mathsf{Perp}^{i}))_{i=1}^{k}\ ,

(85)

where
(\mathfrak{S}^{A,\mathtt{x}}_{1})^{\perp}
is the extended perpendicular
1^{\rm st}

\mathtt{x}
-seeded CLM defined in
Item

(3)
. In addition, check that

\forall i\in[k]\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{1}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})=0\ .

(86)

In other words, if
(\mathtt{x}_{hide\ 1},\nu_{hide\ 1})
is the answer to
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{1}
and
\chi
is the answer to
\mathtt{Pauli}_{{\mathds{X}}}
, check that
\mathtt{x}_{hide\ 1}=\vec{0}
, and that

(\mathfrak{S}^{A,\mathtt{x}_{hide\ 1}}_{1})^{\perp}(\chi)=\nu_{hide\ 1}\ .

(87)

This is the same as for
\chi^{V_{>1}}=\nu_{hide\ 1}^{V_{>1}}
and
(\mathfrak{s}^{A}_{1})^{\perp}(\chi^{V_{1}})=\nu_{hide\ 1}^{V_{1}}
. Note that, as
(\mathfrak{S}_{1}^{A,\mathtt{x}})^{\perp}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}
is a linear map, this check can be tailored appropriately.

(4)

In case
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j}-\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j-1}
(respectively
\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{j}-\mathtt{Hide}_{B}^{j-1}
) is sampled for
2\leq j\leq h
: Let
(\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j},\nu_{hide\ j})
be the answer to
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j}
, and
(\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j-1},\nu_{hide\ j-1})
the answer to
\mathtt{Hide}_{A}^{j-1}
. Fix
\mathtt{x}:=\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j}
as the seed, and note that
\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Que}^{A,\cdot}
are readable variables so we may perform checks that depend non-linearly on them. First check that the
(j-2)^{\rm th}
prefix of
\mathtt{x}
(see (
72
)) is equal to
\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j-1}
, namely that
\mathtt{x}_{\leq j-2}=\mathtt{x}_{hide\ j-1}
, or equivalently

\begin{split}\forall i\in I^{\mathtt{x}}_{<j-1}\ &\colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j-1}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})=\gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})\ ,\\
\forall i\in I^{\mathtt{x}}_{\geq j-1}\ &\colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j-1}\mathsf{Que}^{A,i})=0\ ,\end{split}

(88)

where
I^{\mathtt{x}}_{\cdot}
is the set of indices associated with the seeded register subspace
W^{\mathtt{x}}_{\cdot}
which was defined in (
71
).
In addition, check that
(\mathfrak{S}^{A,\mathtt{x}}_{j})^{\perp}(\nu_{hide\ j-1})=\nu_{hide\ j}
, namely that

\forall i\in I^{\mathtt{x}}_{\neq j}\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i})=\gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j-1}\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i})\ ,

(89)

and

\forall i\in I^{\mathtt{x}}_{j}\ \colon\ \ \gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j}\mathsf{Perp}^{A,i})=\sum_{t\in I_{j}}(\mathfrak{s}^{A,\mathtt{x}}_{j})^{\perp}_{it}\gamma(\mathsf{Hide}^{j-1}\mathsf{Perp}^{A,t})\ .

(90)

Again, as
(\mathfrak{S}^{A,\mathtt{x}}_{j})^{\perp}
is linear for every seed
\mathtt{x}
, and
\mathtt{x}
is decided by readable variables, these checks can be tailored appropriately.

Remark 4.21

(Restriction notation)
.

Recall the notation
\mathcal{V}^{S^{\prime}}
(in observable form) and
\mathcal{Q}^{S^{\prime}}
(in projective form) for the restriction to
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{S^{\prime}}
of the respective PVMs
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{Q}
with outcomes in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{S^{\prime}\sqcup S^{\prime\prime}}
(Definition
3.32
).
For example, in the case of
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
, recall that

{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}={\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\mathfrak{R}}\sqcup\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\mathfrak{L}}}=\{\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\mathfrak{R},j},\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\mathfrak{L},j}\}_{j=1}^{\Lambda}

is the set of
\mathsf{Ans}^{A}
-variables, which is a subset of
S_{\mathtt{Intro}_{A}}
. Then, the representation
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}}\to U(N)
is induced by the observable form
\mathcal{V}
of some strategy
\mathscr{S}
by letting

\forall\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\Lambda}\ \colon\ \ \mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}(\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}},\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}})=\prod_{i}\mathcal{V}(\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\mathfrak{R},i})^{\alpha^{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}\prod_{j}\mathcal{V}(\mathsf{Ans}^{A,\mathfrak{L},j})^{\alpha^{\mathfrak{L}}_{j}}\ ,

and the PVM
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}
is induced by the projective form
\mathcal{Q}
by letting

\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{Ans}^{A}}_{a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}}}=\sum_{\mathtt{x}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r}}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathtt{Intro}_{A}}_{\mathtt{x},a^{\mathfrak{R}},a^{\mathfrak{L}}}\ .

We use a similar notation for restrictions to various subsets of generators at different vertices, such as

\mathsf{ReadQue}^{\cdot},\mathsf{ReadPerp}^{\cdot},\mathsf{SamZ}^{\cdot}\;,

and so on.

Remark 4.22

(Analysis of the question reducred game
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
)
.

Let us briefly analyze the properties of a strategy
\mathscr{S}
, with
\mathcal{U}
being its observable form and
\mathcal{P}
its projective form, that passes all edges but
\mathtt{Intro}_{A}-\mathtt{Intro}_{B}
in
\mathfrak{QueRed}(\mathfrak{G})
perfectly:

(1)

Since it passes the copy of
{\mathfrak{Pauli\ Basis}}_{k}
perfectly, we can deduce by Claim
3.83
and Fact
3.73
(in the case
\varepsilon=0
) that the observables
\mathscr{S}
associates with the generators at the vertices
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{X}}
and
\mathtt{Pauli}_{\mathds{Z}}
induce the unique (up to direct sums) representation of the Pauli group
{\rm P}_{k}
defined in (
45
). Namely, there is some natural number
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathscr{S}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}
, and
